that were returned during the time interval
[\tau,\tau^{\prime})
contained
e
, and for each such path
P_{j}
, edge
e
contributed at least
\tilde{\ell}^{\prime}(e)/2
to the length of the path.
Therefore, every edge
e\in\tilde{E}^{\prime\prime}
contributes at least
\frac{\tilde{\ell}^{\prime}(e)\cdot\eta}{2}
to
\sum_{j=1}^{r}C_{j}
, and so
\sum_{j=1}^{r}C_{j}\geq\sum_{e\in\tilde{E}^{\prime\prime}}\frac{\tilde{\ell}^{\prime}(e)\cdot\eta}{2}
. On the other hand:

\sum_{j=1}^{r}C_{j}\leq r\cdot d_{X_{1}}\leq\Delta\cdot d_{X_{1}}\leq\Delta\cdot\min\left\{\frac{|X_{1}^{0}|\cdot\eta}{\Delta},d\right\}\leq|X_{1}^{0}|\cdot\eta\leq W^{0}(X_{1})\cdot\eta.

Altogether, we get that
\sum_{e\in\tilde{E}^{\prime\prime}}\tilde{\ell}^{\prime}(e)\leq\frac{2}{\eta}\cdot\sum_{j=1}^{r}C_{j}\leq 2W^{0}(X_{1})
, and so:

\sum_{i=1}^{z}W_{i}=\sum_{v\in Y}\tilde{w}^{\prime}(v)\leq 2\sum_{e\in\tilde{E}^{\prime}}\tilde{\ell}^{\prime}(e)\leq 4\sum_{e\in\tilde{E}^{\prime}}\tilde{\ell}(e)+2\sum_{e\in\tilde{E}^{\prime\prime}}\tilde{\ell}^{\prime}(e)\leq 8W^{0}(X_{1}).

Recall that, for every cluster
X\in\tilde{\mathcal{X}}^{\prime}_{2}
,
\frac{\Delta\cdot(\log N)^{128}}{\eta}\leq W^{0}(X)\leq 8n\log^{5}n
holds (see the definition of leaf clusters (
Definition

5.4
) and Inequality
6
). We now prove that, for all
\left\lfloor\log\left(\frac{\Delta\cdot(\log N)^{128}}{\eta}\right)\right\rfloor<j\leq 4\left\lceil\log n\right\rceil
, if
X\in\tilde{\mathcal{X}}^{\prime}_{2}
is a cluster with
2^{j}\leq W^{0}(X)<2^{j+1}
, then
X
has at most
16j
close descendants.

The proof is by induction on
j
. The base case is when
j=\left\lfloor\log\left(\frac{\Delta\cdot(\log N)^{128}}{\eta}\right)\right\rfloor
. Recall that, if
W^{0}(X^{\prime})<\frac{\Delta\cdot(\log N)^{128}}{\eta}
, then
X^{\prime}
is a leaf cluster. Therefore, if
X\in\tilde{\mathcal{X}}^{\prime}_{2}
is a cluster with
2^{j}\leq W^{0}(X)<2^{j+1}
, and
X^{\prime}\in\tilde{\mathcal{X}}_{2}
is its close descendant, then
W^{0}(X^{\prime})\geq\frac{\Delta\cdot(\log N)^{128}}{\eta}\geq\frac{W^{0}(X)}{2}
must hold.
For a cluster
X\in\tilde{\mathcal{X}}_{2}^{\prime}
with
2^{j}\leq W^{0}(X)<2^{j+1}
,
let
{\mathcal{D}}(X)\subseteq\tilde{X}^{\prime}_{2}
be the collection of the close descendants of
X
, and denote
|{\mathcal{D}}(X)|=z
. Then we can define an ordering
(X=X_{1},X_{2},\ldots,X_{z})
of the clusters in
{\mathcal{D}}(X)
, such that, for all
1\leq i<z
,
X_{i+1}
is a child-cluster of
X_{i}
. From
Claim

B.1
, we are then guaranteed that
z\leq 16
.

For the induction step, assume that the claim holds for some integer
j\geq\left\lfloor\log\left(\frac{\Delta\cdot(\log N)^{128}}{\eta}\right)\right\rfloor
, and consider a cluster
X\in\tilde{\mathcal{X}}^{\prime}_{2}
with
2^{j+1}\leq W^{0}(X)<2^{j+2}
. Let
{\mathcal{D}}(X)\subseteq\tilde{X}^{\prime}_{2}
be the set of all close descendants of
X
, and denote
|{\mathcal{D}}(X)|=z
. As before, we can define an ordering
(X=X_{1},X_{2},\ldots,X_{z})
of the clusters in
{\mathcal{D}}(X)
, such that, for all
1\leq i<r
,
X_{i+1}
is a child-cluster of
X_{i}
. Let
i^{\prime}>1
be the smallest index with
W^{0}(X_{i^{\prime}})<W^{0}(X)/2
. Then for all
1\leq i<i^{\prime}
,
W^{0}(X_{i})\geq W^{0}(X)/2
, and so, from
Claim

B.1
,
i^{\prime}\leq 17
. Moreover, clusters
X_{i^{\prime}+1},\ldots,X_{z}
are close descendants of cluster
X_{i^{\prime}}
, and so, from the induction hypothesis,
z-i^{\prime}+1\leq 16j
. Altogether, we get that
z=(z-i^{\prime}+1)+i^{\prime}-1\leq 16(j+1)
. Since, for every cluster
X\in\tilde{\mathcal{X}}_{2}^{\prime}
,
W^{0}(X)\leq 8n\log^{5}n<2^{4\left\lceil\log n\right\rceil}
, we get that each such cluster has at most
64\left\lceil\log n\right\rceil
close descendants.

Appendix C
Proofs Omitted from
Section

6

C.1
Proof of
Claim

6.2

Observe first that, from our definition, graph
H^{\prime}
is a well-structured graph.

Recall that
N\geq\frac{W}{32}
, while
|V(H^{\prime})|\leq|V(H)|+\beta\cdot M\leq 2W\log^{3}N
. Since
N^{\prime}=64N\log^{3}N
,
N^{\prime}\geq|V(H^{\prime})|
holds. Since we have assumed that
k\geq 2^{20}\cdot d^{\prime}\cdot\log^{6}N
, we get that
1\leq\eta^{\prime}\leq\Delta^{\prime}
, as required. Since
U_{\lambda}\subseteq A^{\prime}
, while
B^{\prime}
contains all vertices of
U_{0}
and their copies, it is easy to verify that
|A^{\prime}|,|B^{\prime}|\geq k=\Delta^{\prime}
. Therefore, for any pair
(A^{\prime},B^{\prime})
of disjoint subsets of
V(H^{\prime})
with
U_{\lambda}\subseteq A
, and with all vertices of
U_{0}
and their copies lying in
B^{\prime}
,
(H^{\prime},A^{\prime},B^{\prime},\Delta^{\prime},\eta^{\prime},N^{\prime})
defines a valid instance of the
\mathsf{RouteAndCut}
problem.
We now verify that this instance is
r
-restricted.

In order to do so, it is enough to show that
1\leq r\leq\left\lceil\sqrt{\log N^{\prime}}\right\rceil
and
\frac{(|V(H^{\prime})|-|B^{\prime}|)\cdot\eta^{\prime}}{\Delta^{\prime}}\leq 2^{r\cdot\sqrt{\log N^{\prime}}}
holds. Since
1\leq r\leq\sqrt{\log N}
, we get that
1\leq r\leq\left\lceil\sqrt{\log N^{\prime}}\right\rceil
also holds.

Recall that
|V(H^{\prime})\setminus B^{\prime}|\leq 4\lambda k
. Therefore:

\frac{(|V(H^{\prime})|-|B^{\prime}|)\cdot\eta^{\prime}}{\Delta^{\prime}}\leq\frac{4\lambda k\cdot 2^{20}\cdot d^{\prime}\cdot\log^{6}N}{k}\leq d^{\prime}\cdot\log^{8}N\leq 2^{r\sqrt{\log N}}\leq 2^{r\cdot\sqrt{\log N^{\prime}}},

since
\lambda=\left\lceil\log(64N)\right\rceil+1
and
d^{\prime}\leq\frac{2^{r\sqrt{\log N}}}{\log^{8}N}
from the definition of Procedure
\mathsf{ReconnectLayer}
.

C.2
Proof of
Observation

6.4

We first prove that
|\tilde{Y}|\leq O(k\log N)
. Recall that set
\tilde{Y}
may contain all vertices of
H^{\prime}
, except for the vertices of
B^{\prime}_{j}
. It is easy to verify that
|B^{\prime}_{j}|\geq|B_{j}|-k
, since
|{\mathcal{R}}^{\prime}_{j}|<k
. Recall that we have shown that
|V(H^{\prime})\setminus B_{j}|\leq O(\lambda\cdot k)\leq O(k\log N)
. Therefore,
|\tilde{Y}|\leq|V(H^{\prime})\setminus B_{j}|+|B_{j}\setminus B^{\prime}_{j}|\leq O(k\log N)
.

In order to prove the remaining two assertions, we partition the set
\beta
of vertices into three subsets. The first set,
\beta_{1}
, contains all vertices
x\in\beta
that lie in
\tilde{Y}^{\prime}
. The second set,
\beta_{2}
, contains all vertices
x\in\beta
that lie in
\tilde{Y}
, such that every copy of
x
lies in
\tilde{Y}
as well. The third set,
\beta_{3}
, contains all remaining vertices of
\beta
.

Notice that, for every vertex
x\in\beta_{3}
, some edge that is incident to one of the copies of
x
must lie in
E_{H^{\prime}}(\tilde{Y},\tilde{Y}^{\prime})
, and so
|\beta_{3}|\leq|E_{H^{\prime}}(\tilde{Y},\tilde{Y}^{\prime})|\leq\frac{k}{d^{\prime}\log^{6}N}\leq\frac{|\beta|}{\log^{4}N}
,
since
|\beta|\geq\frac{W}{d^{\prime}\cdot\log^{2}N}\geq\frac{k}{d^{\prime}\cdot\log^{2}N}
.

Notice also that, for every vertex
x\in\beta_{2}
, all
M
copies of
x
(including
x
itself) must serve as endpoints of the paths in
{\mathcal{R}}^{\prime}_{j}
, so
|\beta_{2}|\leq\frac{|{\mathcal{R}}^{\prime}_{j}|}{M}\leq\frac{k\cdot|\beta|}{W\log^{3}N}\leq\min\left\{\frac{k}{\log^{3}N},\frac{|\beta|}{\log^{3}N}\right\}
, since
M=\frac{W\cdot\log^{3}N}{|\beta|}
and
k,|\beta|\leq|V(H)|\leq W
.

Overall, we get that:

|\tilde{Y}^{\prime}\cap\beta|=|\beta_{1}|\geq|\beta|-|\beta_{2}|-|\beta_{3}|\geq|\beta|\cdot\left(1-\frac{1}{\log^{4}N}-\frac{1}{\log^{3}N}\right)\geq|\beta|\cdot\left(1-\frac{2}{\log^{3}N}\right).

Additionally,

|\tilde{Y}^{\prime}\cap\beta|\geq|\beta|-|\beta_{2}|-|\beta_{3}|\geq|\beta|-\frac{k}{\log^{3}N}-\frac{k}{d^{\prime}\log^{6}N}\geq|\beta|-\frac{2k}{\log^{3}N}.

Lastly, since
|A^{\prime}_{j}|\geq\frac{k}{64\lambda}\geq\frac{k}{2^{9}\log N}
, as
\lambda=\left\lceil\log(64N)\right\rceil+1\leq 8\log N
, while
|\beta_{2}|\leq\frac{k}{\log^{3}N}

and
|\beta_{3}|\leq\frac{k}{d^{\prime}\log^{6}N}
, we get that
|A^{\prime}_{j}|\geq(|\beta_{2}|+|\beta_{3}|)\cdot\log N=|\tilde{Y}\cap\beta|\cdot\log N
.

C.3
Proof of
Claim

6.12

Let
\beta^{\prime}_{1}
be the set of vertices that were deleted from
\beta
directly, and let
\beta^{\prime}_{2}=\beta^{\prime}\setminus\beta^{\prime}_{1}
. Since at most one vertex may be deleted from
\beta
directly in every iteration,
|\beta^{\prime}_{1}|\leq\Delta
must hold.

Let
E^{\prime}
denote the set of all special edges whose lengths have increased over the course of the algorithm.
Consider any such edge
e=(u,v)\in E^{\prime}
, and let
\tau
be the first time when the length of
e
was increased. Then at time
\tau
, neither of the vertices
u,v
may lie in
\beta
. Therefore, either both of these vertices lied in
V(G^{0})\setminus\beta^{0}
; or at least one of these vertices lies in
\beta^{\prime}_{1}
(since, once a vertex is added to
\beta_{2}^{\prime}
, it is deleted from
G
). We conclude that
|E^{\prime}|\leq|V(G^{0})|-|\beta^{0}\setminus\beta^{\prime}_{1}|\leq n-|\beta^{0}|+\Delta
.
For every edge
e\in E^{\prime}
, let
\ell^{0}(e)=1
be its initial length.
Clearly,
\sum_{e\in E^{\prime}}\ell^{0}(e)\leq n-|\beta^{0}|+|\beta^{\prime}_{1}|\leq n-|\beta^{0}|+\Delta
.

Denote by
\left\{P_{1},\ldots,P_{q}\right\}
the set of all paths that the algorithm returned in response to the queries. For all
1\leq i\leq q
, let
C_{i}
be the length of path
P_{i}
at the time it was returned, so
C_{i}\leq d
, and
\sum_{i=1}^{q}C_{i}\leq d\cdot q\leq d\cdot\Delta
.

Let
\hat{E}\subseteq E^{\prime}
be the set of edges whose length increased more than once. For every edge
e\in\hat{E}
, let
\ell^{*}(e)
be the final length of the edge
e
, after its last increase, and let
\tau^{\prime}(e)<\tau^{\prime\prime}(e)
be the times of the last two increases in the length of
e
. Then during the time interval
(\tau^{\prime}(e),\tau^{\prime\prime}(e)]
, at least
\eta
paths that were returned in response to the queries contained
e
, and edge
e
contributed at least
\frac{\ell^{*}(e)}{2}
to the length
C_{i}
of each such path
P_{i}
. In other words, every edge
e\in\hat{E}
contributes at least
\frac{\eta\cdot\ell^{*}(e)}{2}
to
\sum_{i=1}^{q}C_{i}
. Therefore:

\sum_{i=1}^{q}C_{i}\geq\frac{\eta}{2}\sum_{e\in\hat{E}}\ell^{*}(e),

and:

\sum_{e\in\hat{E}}\ell^{*}(e)\leq\frac{2}{\eta}\cdot\sum_{i=1}^{q}C_{i}\leq\frac{2d\Delta}{\eta}.

Altogether, we get that:

\begin{split}\Lambda&\leq\sum_{e\in E^{\prime}}2\ell^{0}(e)+\sum_{e\in\hat{E}}\ell^{*}(e)\\
&\leq 2n-2|\beta^{0}|+2\Delta+\frac{2d\Delta}{\eta}\\
&\leq 4n-4|\beta^{0}|+3\Delta\cdot(\log N)^{64}.\end{split}

(we have used the fact that, from the problem definition,
\frac{\Delta\cdot d}{\eta}\leq|V(G)|-|\beta^{0}|+\Delta\cdot(\log N)^{64}
). Since, from the problem definition,
\Delta\leq\frac{|\beta^{0}|}{(\log N)^{128}}
, we get that
\Lambda\leq 4n-4|\beta^{0}|+3\Delta\cdot(\log N)^{64}\leq 4n
.

Next, we bound
|\beta^{\prime}_{2}|
. Denote
\hat{\beta}=\beta^{0}\setminus\beta^{\prime}_{1}
, and recall that
|\hat{\beta}|\geq|\beta^{0}|-\Delta
. For every vertex
v\in V(G^{0})
, let
w^{0}(v)=1
denote the initial weight of
v
, and let
w^{*}(v)
denote the weight of
v
at the end of the algorithm (if
v\in V(G)
holds), or just before
v
is deleted from
G
(otherwise). We also denote by
\tilde{W}=\sum_{v\in V(G^{0})\setminus\hat{\beta}}w^{*}(v)
.

Notice that, at the beginning of the algorithm:

\sum_{v\in V(G^{0})\setminus\hat{\beta}}w^{0}(v)=n-|\hat{\beta}|\leq n-|\beta^{0}|+\Delta.

Since the total increase in all edge lengths is
\Lambda\leq 4n-4|\beta^{0}|+5\Delta\cdot(\log N)^{64}
, we get that:

\begin{split}\tilde{W}\leq\sum_{v\in V(G^{0})\setminus\hat{\beta}}w^{0}(v)+2\Lambda\leq 9n-9|\beta^{0}|+11\Delta\cdot(\log N)^{64}.\end{split}

Consider now any strongly well-structured cut
(X,Y)
in
G
of sparsity
\Phi_{G}(X,Y)\leq\frac{(\log N)^{64}}{d}
, such that
w(X)\geq 1.8|\beta\cap X|
holds, that the algorithm produces. Let
J^{\prime}\subseteq Y
denote the set of vertices that serve as endpoints of the edges of
E_{G}(X,Y)
, and recall that
|J^{\prime}|\leq|E_{G}(X,Y)|\leq\frac{w(X)\cdot(\log N)^{64}}{d}\leq w(X)
, since
d\geq(\log N)^{64}
from the problem definition. Note that
\hat{\beta}\cap X\subseteq\beta\cap X
, and so
w(X)\geq 1.8|\beta\cap X|\geq 1.8|\hat{\beta}\cap X|
holds. Since the weights of all vertices in
\hat{\beta}
are
1
, we get that:

w(X\setminus\hat{\beta})=w(X)-|X\cap\hat{\beta}|\geq w(X)\left(1-\frac{1}{1.8}\right)\geq 0.44w(X).

The number of vertices that were indirectly deleted due to the cut is bounded by:

|X\cap\hat{\beta}|+|J^{\prime}|\leq 2w(X)\leq 5w(X\setminus\hat{\beta})\leq 5\sum_{v\in X\setminus\hat{\beta}}w^{*}(v).

We assign to every vertex
v\in X\setminus\hat{\beta}
a
charge
of
5w^{*}(v)
units, so that the total charge to all vertices of
X\setminus\hat{\beta}
is greater than the number of vertices that are deleted from
\beta
in the current iteration. Notice that the vertices of
X\setminus\hat{\beta}
are deleted from
G
, and so they will never be charged again. We conclude that:

|\beta^{\prime}_{2}|\leq 5\hat{W}\leq 45n-45|\beta^{0}|+55\Delta\cdot(\log N)^{64}.

Altogether, we get that
|\beta^{\prime}|\leq|\beta^{\prime}_{1}|+|\beta^{\prime}_{2}|\leq 45n-45|\beta^{0}|+56\Delta\cdot(\log N)^{64}
.

C.4
Proof of
Corollary

6.13

First, observe that:

\sum_{v\in V(G)\setminus\beta}w(v)\leq|V(G)|-|\beta|+2\Lambda\leq|V(G)|-|\beta|+8(n-|\beta^{0}|)+10\Delta(\log N)^{64},

from
Claim

6.12
.
Notice that, if
v\in V(G)\setminus\beta
, then either
v\in V(G^{0})\setminus\beta^{0}
; or
v
belonged to
\beta
, but was directly removed from
\beta
. Since the number of vertices that are directly removed from
\beta
is bounded by
\Delta
, and since, from the problem definition,
\Delta\leq\frac{|\beta^{0}|}{(\log N)^{128}}
, we get that:

\sum_{v\in V(G)\setminus\beta}w(v)\leq 9(n-|\beta^{0}|)+11\Delta(\log N)^{64}\leq 9(n-|\beta^{0}|)+\frac{|\beta^{0}|}{1000}.

Lastly, since
|\beta^{0}|\geq\frac{99n}{100}
from the problem definition, we get that
(n-|\beta^{0}|)\leq\frac{n}{100}\leq\frac{|\beta^{0}|}{99}
.
Using the fact that
|\beta|\geq\frac{|\beta^{0}|}{2}
holds throughout the algorithm, we now get that:

\sum_{v\in V(G)\setminus\beta}w(v)\leq\frac{9|\beta^{0}|}{99}+\frac{|\beta^{0}|}{1000}\leq 0.1|\beta^{0}|\leq 0.2|\beta|.

C.5
Proof of
Observation

6.14

First, from
Corollary

6.13
,
w(A\setminus\beta)\leq\sum_{v\in V(G)\setminus\beta}w(v)\leq 0.2|\beta|
.

On the other hand,
w(A\setminus\beta)=w(A)-w(A\cap\beta)=w(A)-|A\cap\beta|\geq w(A)\left(1-\frac{1}{1.8}\right)\geq 0.44w(A)
. Therefore,
w(A)\leq 2.27w(A\setminus\beta)\leq 0.46|\beta|
. In particular,
|\beta\cap A|\leq w(A)\leq 0.46|\beta|
. but then:

w(B)\geq|B\cap\beta|\geq|\beta|-|A\cap\beta|\geq 0.54|\beta|>w(A).

Appendix D
Proofs Omitted from
Section

7

D.1
Proof of
Observation

7.2

Let
{\mathcal{P}}
be the set of all paths that the algorithm returned in response to queries.
For every path
P\in{\mathcal{P}}
, we denote by
\ell^{\prime}(P)
the length of the path
P
at the time when it was returned, so
\ell^{\prime}(P)\leq d
. Since
|{\mathcal{P}}|\leq\Delta
, we get that
\sum_{P\in{\mathcal{P}}}\ell^{\prime}(P)\leq d\cdot\Delta
.

Let
E^{\prime}\subseteq E^{\mathsf{spec}}
be the set of all edges
e
with
\ell^{*}(e)>2\ell^{0}(e)
. Consider any such edge
e\in E^{\prime}
, and denote
\ell^{*}(e)=2^{i}
. Let
\tau
be the first time when the length of
e
was set to
2^{i-1}
, and let
\tau^{\prime}
be the time when the length of
e
was set to
2^{i}
. Then, in time interval
(\tau,\tau^{\prime}]
, at least
\eta
paths were added to
{\mathcal{P}}
that contained
e
, and, for each such path
P
, edge
e
contributed at least
2^{i-1}=\frac{\ell^{*}(e)}{2}
to
\ell^{\prime}(P)
.
Therefore:

\sum_{P\in{\mathcal{P}}}\ell^{\prime}(P)\geq\sum_{e\in E^{\prime}}\frac{\ell^{*}(e)\cdot\eta}{2}.

Since
\sum_{P\in{\mathcal{P}}}\ell^{\prime}(P)\leq d\cdot\Delta
, we get that
\sum_{e\in E^{\prime}}\ell^{*}(e)\leq\frac{2d\Delta}{\eta}
.
Altogether, we get that:

\sum_{e\in E^{\mathsf{spec}}}\ell^{*}(e)\leq 2\sum_{e\in E^{\mathsf{spec}}}\ell^{0}(e)+\sum_{e\in E^{\prime}}\ell^{*}(e)\leq 2\sum_{e\in E^{\mathsf{spec}}}\ell^{0}(e)+\frac{2d\Delta}{\eta}\leq 2\sum_{e\in E^{\mathsf{spec}}}\ell^{0}(e)+2n,

since
\frac{\Delta d}{\eta}\leq n
from the problem definition.

D.2
Proof of
Claim

7.9

Recall that
(A_{1},B_{1}),\ldots,(A_{q},B_{q})
is the sequence of all stronlgy well-structured
\varphi
-sparse cuts that our algorithm produced during the current phase so far, indexed in the order in which the algorithm produced them. For all
1\leq i\leq q
, denote by
\tau_{i}
the time when cut
(A_{i},B_{i})
was computed, by
E_{i}=E_{G}(A_{i},B_{i})
, and by
J_{i}\subseteq A_{i},J^{\prime}_{i}\subseteq B_{i}
the vertices that serve as endpoints of the edges in
E_{i}
(with respect to
G^{(\tau_{i})}
– graph
G
at time
\tau_{i}
). If, at time
\tau_{i}
,
w(A_{i})>w(B_{i})
, then we denote
Z_{i}=A_{i}\setminus J_{i}
and
\overline{Z}_{i}=B_{i}\setminus J^{\prime}_{i}
; otherwise, we denote
Z_{i}=B_{i}\setminus J_{i}^{\prime}
and
\overline{Z}_{i}=A_{i}\setminus J_{i}
. In the former case, we say that cut
(A_{i},B_{i})
is of
type 1
, and in the latter case it is of
type 2
.

We denote by
G_{0}
the graph
G
at the beginning of the phase, and, for all
1\leq i\leq q
, we denote by
G_{i}
the graph
G
that is obtained after cut
(A_{i},B_{i})
has been processed. Therefore, for all
1\leq i\leq q
, graph
G_{i}
is obtained from graph
G_{i-1}
by deleting the vertices of
\overline{Z}_{i}\cup J_{i}\cup J^{\prime}_{i}
from it, or, equivalently,
G_{i}=G_{i-1}[Z_{i}]
. The current graph
G
is then denoted by
G_{q}
, and we denote the current graph
H
by
H_{q}
. We also let
E^{\prime}=\bigcup_{j=1}^{q}E_{j}
.

Since the total number of vertices deleted so far from
H
during the current phase is less than
\frac{\hat{W}}{\log^{30}N}
, and since, for all
1\leq j\leq q
,
|E_{j}|\leq\varphi\cdot\min\left\{w(A_{j}),w(B_{j})\right\}
, we conclude that:

|E^{\prime}|\leq\sum_{j=1}^{q}|E_{j}|\leq\frac{\varphi\cdot\hat{W}}{\log^{30}N}=\frac{4\hat{W}}{d^{\prime}\cdot\log^{30}N}\leq\frac{8|T|}{\log^{30}N},

(21)

since
|T|\geq\frac{\hat{W}}{2d^{\prime}}
, as Event
{\cal{E}}_{1}
did not happen.

Our first step is to compute a partition of the vertices of
G_{0}
into three subsets,
S_{1},S_{2},S_{3}
, such that
S_{2}=V(G_{q})
, and the partition has several other useful properties, summarized in the following claim.

Claim D.1
.

There is a deterministic algorithm, that computes a partition
(S_{1},S_{2},S_{3})
of the vertices of
G_{0}
, such that the following hold:

•

S_{2}=V(G_{q})
;

•

if
e=(x,y)
is an edge of
G_{0}
connecting a vertex
x\in S_{j}
to a vertex
y\in S_{i}
with
1\leq j<i\leq 3
, then
e
is a special edge, and moreover,
e\in E^{\prime}
holds; and

•

if
e=(x,y)
is an edge of
G_{0}
connecting a vertex
x\in S_{j}
to a vertex
y\in S_{i}
with
1\leq i<j\leq 3
, then
e
is a regular edge.

The running time of the algorithm is
O((\hat{W})^{2})
.

Proof.

We start by constructing a sequence
{\mathcal{S}}=(Y_{1},\ldots,Y_{q+1})
of subsets of vertices of
G_{0}
, over the course of
q
iterations.
Specifically, for all
1\leq i\leq q
, in iteration
i
we will construct a sequence
{\mathcal{S}}_{i}

of
i+1
subsets of vertices of
G_{0}
. We will also identify a single set
S_{i}^{*}\in{\mathcal{S}}_{i}
as the
distingiushed
vertex set of
{\mathcal{S}}_{i}
, and we will define a subset
J_{i}^{*}\subseteq S_{i}^{*}
of vertices of
S_{i}^{*}
called
boundary
vertices. We require that the following properties hold.

R1.

The sets of vertices in
{\mathcal{S}}_{i}
define a partition of
V(G_{0})
;

R2.

The set
J^{*}_{i}
of boundary vertices contains all vertices of
S^{*}_{i}
that serve as endpoints of the edges of
\bigcup_{i^{\prime}=1}^{i}E_{i^{\prime}}
and only them;

R3.

V(G_{i})=S_{i}^{*}\setminus J_{i}^{*}
;

R4.

For every boundary vertex
v\in J_{i}^{*}
, every edge incident to
v
in
G_{0}[S^{*}_{i}]
is a regular edge;

R5.

If
S,S^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{S}}_{i}
, with
S
appearing before
S^{\prime}
in the sequence, and
e
is an edge of
G_{0}
connecting a vertex of
S^{\prime}
to a vertex of
S
, then
e
is a regular edge of
G_{0}
; and

R6.

If
S,S^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{S}}_{i}
, with
S
appearing before
S^{\prime}
in the sequence, and
e
is an edge of
G_{0}
connecting a vertex of
S
to a vertex of
S^{\prime}
, then
e\in\bigcup_{i^{\prime}=1}^{i}E_{i^{\prime}}
.

In the first iteration, we consider the cut
(A_{1},B_{1})
, and we let the initial sequence be
{\mathcal{S}}_{1}=(A_{1},B_{1})
. Notice that every edge connecting a vertex of
A_{1}
to a vertex of
B_{1}
is an edge of
E_{1}
, and, since cut
(A_{1},B_{1})
is strongly well-structured, every edge connecting a vertex of
B_{1}
to a vertex of
A_{1}
is a regular edge.
This immediately establishes Properties
R1
,
R5
and
R6
for
{\mathcal{S}}_{1}
.

If
(A_{1},B_{1})
is a type-1 cut, that is,
w(A_{1})>w(B_{1})
, then we designate
A_{1}
as the distinguished set
S^{*}_{1}
of vertices for
{\mathcal{S}}_{1}
, and we let
J_{1}
be the corresponding set
J^{*}_{1}
of boundary vertices.
Alternatively, if
(A_{1},B_{1})
is a type-2 cut, then we designate
B_{1}
as the distinguished set
S^{*}_{1}
of vertices for
{\mathcal{S}}_{1}
, and we let
J^{\prime}_{1}
be the corresponding set
J^{*}_{1}
of boundary vertices.
It is immediate to verify that, with this definition, Properties
R2
–
R4
hold.

Assume now that for some
1\leq i<q
, we have computed the sequence
{\mathcal{S}}_{i}=(S_{1},S_{2},\ldots,S_{i+1})
, for which Properties
R1
–
R6
hold. Consider the distinguished set
S^{*}_{i}
of
{\mathcal{S}}_{i}
that we denote for simplicity by
S^{*}
, and its corresponding set
J^{*}
of boundary vertices that we denote by
J^{*}
. Recall that, from Requirement
R3
,
V(G_{i})=S^{*}\setminus J^{*}
. Consider now the cut
(A_{i+1},B_{i+1})
in
G_{i}
. We extend this cut to a partition
(A^{\prime},B^{\prime})
of
S^{*}
as follows. Initially, we set
(A^{\prime},B^{\prime})=(A_{i+1},B_{i+1})
. Next, we consider the boundary vertices
v\in J^{*}
one by one. Recall that, from Property
R4
, only regular edges may be incident to
v
in
G_{0}[S^{*}]
. Therefore, if
v\in L
, then it has no incoming edges in
G_{0}[S^{*}]
; in this case we add it to
B^{\prime}
. Similarly, if
v\in R
, then it has no outgoing edges in
G_{0}[S^{*}]
; in this case we add it to
A^{\prime}
. Once all vertices of
J^{*}
are processed, we obtain the partition
(A^{\prime},B^{\prime})
of
S^{*}
. Notice that
E_{G_{0}}(A^{\prime},B^{\prime})=E_{i+1}
, and all edges of
E_{G_{0}}(B^{\prime},A^{\prime})
are regular edges. In order to obtain sequence
{\mathcal{S}}_{i+1}
, we replace the set
S^{*}
in
{\mathcal{S}}_{i}
with the sets
A^{\prime},B^{\prime}
, in this order. It is immediate to verify that Properties
R1
,
R5
and
R6
continue to hold for
{\mathcal{S}}_{i+1}
.

If cut
(A_{i+1},B_{i+1})
is a type-1 cut, that is,
w(A_{i+1})>w(B_{i+1})
, then we designate
A^{\prime}
as the distinguished set
S^{*}_{i+1}
of vertices for
{\mathcal{S}}_{i+1}
, and we let
J_{i+1}\cup(A^{\prime}\cap J^{*})
be the corresponding set
J^{*}_{i+1}
of boundary vertices.
Alternatively, if
(A_{i+1},B_{i+1})
is a type-2 cut, then we designate
B^{\prime}
as the distinguished set
S^{*}_{i+1}
of vertices for
{\mathcal{S}}_{i+1}
, and we let
J^{\prime}_{i+1}\cup(B^{\prime}\cap J^{*})
be the corresponding set
J^{*}_{i+1}
of boundary vertices.
It is immediate to verify that, with this definition, Properties
R2
–
R4
continue to hold.
The algorithm consists of
q\leq O(\hat{W})
iterations, and each iteration can be executed in time
O(\hat{W})
. Therefore, the running time of the algorithm is
O(\hat{W}^{2})
.

We are now ready to define the partition
(S_{1},S_{2},S_{3})
of the vertices of
G_{0}
. Consider the final sequence
{\mathcal{Y}}_{q}=(Y_{1},\ldots,Y_{q+1})
, and assume that its distinguished set is
Y_{j}
. We denote by
J^{*}
the set of the boundary vertices of
Y_{j}
. Initially, we set
S_{1}=\bigcup_{j^{\prime}=1}^{j-1}Y_{j^{\prime}}
,
S_{2}=Y_{j}
, and
S_{3}=\bigcup_{j^{\prime}=j+1}^{q}Y_{j^{\prime}}
. Clearly,
(S_{1},S_{2},S_{3})
is a partition of
V(G_{0})
, and, from properties
R5
and
R6
, for every pair
1\leq i<i^{\prime}\leq 3
of indices, for every edge
e=(x,y)
with
x\in S_{i},y\in S_{i^{\prime}}
,
e
must be a special edge that lies in
E^{\prime}
, while for every edge
e^{\prime}=(x^{\prime},y^{\prime})
with
x^{\prime}\in S_{i^{\prime}},y^{\prime}\in S_{i}
, edge
e^{\prime}
is a regular edge. Additionally, from Property
R3
,
S_{2}=V(G_{q})\cup J^{*}
. In our last step we will move every vertex of
J^{*}
to either
S_{1}
or
S_{3}
, in order to ensure that
S_{2}=V(G_{q})
holds.

For convenience, if
e=(x,y)
is an edge of
G_{0}
, with
x\in S_{i}
and
y\in S_{i^{\prime}}
, then we say that
e
is a
left-to-right
edge if
i<i^{\prime}
, and that it is a
right-to-left
edge if
i^{\prime}<i
. Notice that the statement of the claim requires that all left-to-right edges are special edges that lie in
E^{\prime}
, and all right-to-left edges are regular edges.

We consider every vertex
v\in J^{*}
one by one.
Recall that, from Property
R4
, all edges incident to
v
in
G_{0}[Y_{j}]
are regular edges. Assume first that
v\in L
. Let
e=(u,v)
be the unique special edge incident to
v
. Then
u\in S_{1}
must hold, and
e
is the only edge entering
v
. We move
v
from
S_{2}
to
S_{3}
. Notice that, if
e^{\prime}
is a regular edge incident to
v
, then
e^{\prime}=(v,u^{\prime})
must hold for some vertex
u^{\prime}
, and so this move does not create any new left-to-right edges, while it may only introduce new right-to-left edges that are regualr edges.

Assume now that
v\in R
. Let
e=(v,u)
be the unique special edge incident to
v
. Then we are guaranteed that
u\in S_{3}
must hold. Edge
e
is the only edge that leaves
v
in
G_{0}
, and all other edges incident to
v
are incoming edges, which must also be regular edges. We move vertex
v
from
S_{2}
to
S_{1}
. As before, this move does not introduce any new left-to-right edges, and the only new right-to-left edges that it may introduce are regular edges that are incident to
v
.

Once every vertex of
J^{*}
is processed we obtain the partition
(S_{1},S_{2},S_{3})
with the required properties. It is easy to verify that the running time of the algorithm is
O((\hat{W})^{2})
.

Note that our updates to the graph
G
after each cut
(A_{i},B_{i})
ensure that
G
remains a perfect well-structured graph throughout the algorithm, so in particular, for every vertex
v\in S_{2}=V(G_{q})
, if
e
is the unique special edge that is incident to
v
in
G_{0}
, then its other endpoint
u
also lies in
S_{2}
. The partition
(S_{1},S_{2},S_{3})
of the vertices of
G_{0}
naturally defines a partition
(\tilde{S}_{1},\tilde{S}_{2},\tilde{S}_{3})
of the vertices of
H_{0}
, as follows. First, for every vertex
v\in V(G_{0})
, if
v\in S_{i}
, for
1\leq i\leq 3
, then we add
v
to
\tilde{S}_{i}
as well. Next, we consider the special edges of
E(G_{0})
. Consider any such special edge
e=(x,y)
, and assume that
x\in S_{i}
and
y\in S_{i^{\prime}}
, for indices
1\leq i,i^{\prime}\leq 3
. If
i=i^{\prime}
, then we add all vertices lying on the path
P^{*}(e)
to set
\tilde{S}_{i}
. Otherwise, from our construction
i<i^{\prime}
must hold, and moreover, from the above discussion,
i=1
and
i^{\prime}=3
must hold. We then add all inner vertices of
P^{*}(e)
to set
\tilde{S}_{1}
. Consider the resulting partition
(\tilde{S}_{1},\tilde{S}_{2},\tilde{S}_{3})
of the vertices of
V(H_{0})
. As before, if
e=(x,y)
is any edge of
H_{0}
with
x\in\tilde{S}_{i}
and
y\in\tilde{S}_{i^{\prime}}
, then we say that
e
is a
left-to-right
edge if
i<i^{\prime}
and we say that it is a
right-to-left
edge if
i^{\prime}<i
. Our construction insures that, if
e^{\prime}
is a left-to-right edge of
H_{0}
with respect to the partition
(\tilde{S}_{1},\tilde{S}_{2},\tilde{S}_{3})
, then its parent-edge
e
is a left-to-right edge of
G_{0}
with respect to the partition
(S_{1},S_{2},S_{3})
. Moreover, if
e
is a left-to-right edge of
G_{0}
with respect to the partition
(S_{1},S_{2},S_{3})
, then exactly one edge of
P^{*}(e)
is a left-to-right edge of
H_{0}
with respect to the partition
(\tilde{S}_{1},\tilde{S}_{2},\tilde{S}_{3})
. We conclude that the total number of left-to-right edges in
H_{0}
with respect to the partition
(\tilde{S}_{1},\tilde{S}_{2},\tilde{S}_{3})

is bounded by
|E^{\prime}|\leq\frac{8|T|}{\log^{30}N}
.
Notice that, since the phase has not terminated so far, the total number of vertices that were deleted from
H_{0}
since the beginning of the phase is at most
\frac{\hat{W}}{\log^{30}N}
, and so in particular,
|\tilde{S}_{1}|+|\tilde{S}_{3}|\leq\frac{\hat{W}}{\log^{30}N}
.

Next, we consider two cases. The first case happens if
|\tilde{S}_{2}\cap T|\leq\left(1-\frac{4}{\log^{4}N}\right)\cdot|T|
. In this case, either
|\tilde{S}_{1}\cap T|\geq\frac{2|T|}{\log^{4}N}
, or
|\tilde{S}_{3}\cap T|\geq\frac{2|T|}{\log^{4}N}
.
Assume first that the former holds. In this case, we return the cut
(\tilde{S},\tilde{S}^{\prime})
, where
\tilde{S}=\tilde{S}_{1}
and
\tilde{S}^{\prime}=\tilde{S}_{2}\cup\tilde{S}_{3}
. From our discussion,
|E_{H_{0}}(\tilde{S},\tilde{S}^{\prime})|\leq\frac{8|T|}{\log^{30}N}\leq\frac{|T|}{\log^{6}N}
. Since
|\tilde{S}_{1}|\leq\frac{\hat{W}}{\log^{30}N}
and
|\tilde{S}_{1}\cap T|\geq\frac{2}{\log^{4}N}
, we get that
\tilde{S}^{\prime}
contains all but at most
\frac{\hat{W}}{\log^{30}N}\leq\frac{\hat{W}}{8}
vertices of
H_{0}
, and at most
\left(1-\frac{2}{\log^{4}N}\right)|T|
vertices of
T
.

Similarly, if
|\tilde{S}_{3}\cap T|\geq\frac{2|T|}{\log^{4}N}
, then we return the cut
(\tilde{S},\tilde{S}^{\prime})
, where
\tilde{S}=\tilde{S}_{1}\cup\tilde{S}_{2}
and
\tilde{S}^{\prime}=\tilde{S}_{3}
. Using the same reasoning as before, it is easy to see that
\tilde{S}
contains all but at most
\frac{\hat{W}}{\log^{30}N}\leq\frac{\hat{W}}{8}
vertices of
H_{0}
, and at most
\left(1-\frac{2}{\log^{4}N}\right)|T|
vertices of
T
.

It now remains to consider the second case, where
|\tilde{S}_{2}\cap T|>\left(1-\frac{4}{\log^{4}N}\right)\cdot|T|
. Intuitively, we would now like to use the finalizing cut
(\tilde{A},\tilde{B})
in graph
H_{q}
, in order to compute the cut
(\tilde{S},\tilde{S}^{\prime})
. For example, we could start with
\tilde{S}=\tilde{S}_{1}
and
\tilde{S}^{\prime}=\tilde{S}_{3}
, and then add vertices of
\tilde{A}
to
\tilde{S}
, and vertices of
\tilde{B}
to
\tilde{S}^{\prime}
. The technical issue with this approach is that
\tilde{S}_{2}
is a subset of vertices of graph
H_{0}
. Specifically,
\tilde{S}_{2}
is the set of vertices of the subdivided graph of
G_{0}[S_{2}]
. Intuitively, we can think of graph
H_{q}
as being obtained from
H_{0}[\tilde{S}_{2}]
by iteratively subdividing some of the special edges. Therefore, not all vertices of
H_{q}
lie in
\tilde{S}_{2}
, and so it is not immediately clear how to use the cut
(\tilde{A},\tilde{B})
in order to obtain the cut
(\tilde{S},\tilde{S}^{\prime})
in graph
H_{0}
with the desired properties. In order to overcome this technical difficulty, we will slightly modify the cut
(\tilde{A},\tilde{B})
in
H_{q}
. The goal of this modification is to ensure that, for every special edge
e\in E(G_{q})
, all inner vertices of
P^{*}(e)
lie on the same side of the cut, and, if both endpoints of
e
lie on the same side of the cut, then so do all vertices of
P^{*}(e)
. At the same time we need to ensure that we preserve some of the useful properties of the cut
(\tilde{A},\tilde{B})
: namely, that
|E_{H_{q}}(\tilde{A},\tilde{B})|
is small, and that one of the sides of the cut contains many vertices of
H_{q}
but relatively few vertices of
T
.

We provide the algorithm for modifying the cut
(\tilde{A},\tilde{B})
in the next observation. For simplicity, in the observation and its proof we denote the graph
H_{q}
by
H
and
G_{q}
by
G
.

Observation D.2
.

There is a deterministic algorithm, that, given a finalizing cut
(\tilde{A},\tilde{B})
in graph
H
, computes a cut
(\tilde{A}^{\prime},\tilde{B}^{\prime})
in
H
, such that the following hold:

•

for each special edge
e=(x,y)
of
G
, either all inner vertices on
P^{*}(e)
lie in
\tilde{A}^{\prime}
, or all inner vertices of
P^{*}(e)
lie in
\tilde{B}^{\prime}
. Moreover, if both endpoints of
e
lie in
\tilde{A}^{\prime}
, then so do all vertices of
P^{*}(e)
, and if both endpoitns of
e
lie in
\tilde{B}^{\prime}
, then so do all vertices of
P^{*}(e)
:

•

all edges in
E_{H}(\tilde{A}^{\prime},\tilde{B}^{\prime})
are special edges;

•

|E_{H}(\tilde{A}^{\prime},\tilde{B}^{\prime})|\leq\frac{|T|}{8\log^{6}N}
; and

•

one of the sides of the cut
\tilde{Z}\in\left\{\tilde{A}^{\prime},\tilde{B}^{\prime}\right\}
contains at least
|V(H)|-\frac{\hat{W}}{16}
vertices of
H
, and at most
|T|\cdot\left(1-\frac{6}{\log^{4}N}\right)
vertices of
T
.

The running time of the algorithm is
O(\hat{W}^{2})
.

We complete the proof of
Observation

D.2
below, after we complete the proof of
Claim

7.9
using it.
Consider some special edge
e\in E(G_{q})
. For simplicity, we denote the subdivided path
P^{*}(e)
in the corresponding graph
H_{q}
by
P^{\prime}(e)
, while we use
P^{*}(e)
to denote the subdivided path corresponding to
e
in
H_{0}
. Therefore,
P^{\prime}(e)
is obtained from
P^{*}(e)
by iteratively subdividing some of the special edges.

We construct the cut
(\tilde{S},\tilde{S}^{\prime})
in graph
H_{0}
as follows. We start with
\tilde{S}=\tilde{S}_{1}
and
\tilde{S}^{\prime}=\tilde{S}_{3}
. Next, we consider all vertices
v\in S_{2}=V(G_{q})
. For each such vertex, if
v\in\tilde{A}^{\prime}
, then we place
v
in
\tilde{S}
, and otherwise, we place it in
\tilde{S}^{\prime}
. Lastly, we consider every special edge
e\in E(G_{q})
. If all inner vertices of
P^{\prime}(e)
lie in
\tilde{A}^{\prime}
, then we place all inner vertices of
P^{*}(e)
in
\tilde{S}
; otherwise, we place all inner vertices of
P^{*}(e)
in
\tilde{S}^{\prime}
.

We now verify that the resulting cut
(\tilde{S},\tilde{S})
has all required properties. Consider first any edge
e\in E_{H_{0}}(\tilde{S},\tilde{S}^{\prime})
. If this edge is incident to a vertex of
\tilde{S}_{1}
or to a vertex of
\tilde{S}_{2}
, then we are guaranteed that
e\in E^{\prime}
holds. Otherwise, there is a parent-edge
e^{\prime}=(x,y)\in G_{0}
of
e
, with
x\in\tilde{A}^{\prime}
,
y\in\tilde{B}^{\prime}
. In this case, path
P^{\prime}(e^{\prime})
contributed exactly one special edge to the set
E_{H_{q}}(\tilde{A}^{\prime},\tilde{B}^{\prime})
in
H_{q}
, and path
P^{*}(e^{\prime})
contributes exactly one special edge to the set
E_{H_{0}}(\tilde{S},\tilde{S}^{\prime})
– the edge
e
. Therefore, every edge in
E_{H_{0}}(\tilde{S},\tilde{S}^{\prime})
is a special edge, and:

|E_{H_{0}}(\tilde{S},\tilde{S}^{\prime})|\leq|E^{\prime}|+|E_{H}(\tilde{A}^{\prime},\tilde{B}^{\prime})|\leq\frac{8|T|}{\log^{30}N}+\frac{|T|}{8\log^{6}N}\leq\frac{|T|}{4\log^{6}N}.

Recall that one of the sides of the cut
\tilde{Z}\in\left\{\tilde{A}^{\prime},\tilde{B}^{\prime}\right\}
contains all but at most
\frac{\hat{W}}{16}
vertices of
H_{q}
, and at most
|T|\cdot\left(1-\frac{6}{\log^{4}N}\right)
vertices of
T
.

Assume first that
\tilde{Z}=\tilde{A}^{\prime}
. Recall that we have established that
|\tilde{S}_{3}|\leq\frac{\hat{W}}{\log^{30}N}
. Since
|\tilde{B}^{\prime}|\leq\frac{\hat{W}}{16}
, we get that
|\tilde{S}^{\prime}|\leq|\tilde{S}_{3}|+|\tilde{B}^{\prime}|\leq\frac{\hat{W}}{\log^{30}N}+\frac{\hat{W}}{16}\leq\frac{\hat{W}}{8}
. Additionally, since we have assumed that
|\tilde{S}_{2}\cap T|>\left(1-\frac{4}{\log^{4}N}\right)\cdot|T|
, we get that
|\tilde{S}_{1}\cap T|\leq\frac{4|T|}{\log^{4}N}
, and so
|\tilde{S}\cap T|\leq|\tilde{S}_{1}\cap T|+|\tilde{A}^{\prime}\cap T|\leq\left(1-\frac{2}{\log^{4}N}\right)\cdot|T|
.

Similarly, if
\tilde{Z}=\tilde{B}^{\prime}
, then
|\tilde{S}|\leq|\tilde{S}_{1}|+|\tilde{A}^{\prime}|\leq\frac{\hat{W}}{\log^{30}N}+\frac{\hat{W}}{16}\leq\frac{\hat{W}}{8}
. Since
we have assumed that
|\tilde{S}_{2}\cap T|>\left(1-\frac{4}{\log^{4}N}\right)\cdot|T|
, we get that
|\tilde{S}_{3}\cap T|\leq\frac{4|T|}{\log^{4}N}
, and so
|\tilde{S}^{\prime}\cap T|\leq|\tilde{S}_{3}\cap T|+|\tilde{B}^{\prime}\cap T|\leq\left(1-\frac{2}{\log^{4}N}\right)\cdot|T|
.

We now analyze the running time of the algorithm. The running times of the algorithms from
Claim

D.1
and from
Observation

D.2
are bounded by
O(\hat{W}^{2})
. It is easy to verify that the running time of the remainder of the algorithm is bounded by
O(\hat{W}^{2})
as well, so that total running time of the algorithm is bounded by
O(\hat{W}^{2})
.

In order to complete the proof of
Claim

7.9
, it is now enough to prove
Observation

D.2
, which we do next. In the proof of the observation we refer to graph
H_{q}
as
H
, and to graph
G_{q}
as
G
.

Proof of
Observation

D.2
.

We partition the set of all special edges of
E(G)
into four subsets. The first set,
E^{A}
, contains all special edges
(x,y)
with
x,y\in\tilde{A}
. The second set,
E^{B}
, contains all special edges
(x,y)
with
x,y\in\tilde{B}
. The third set,
E^{AB}
contains all special edges
(x,y)
with
x\in\tilde{A}
and
y\in\tilde{B}
, and the fourth set,
E^{BA}
, contains all special edges
(x,y)
with
x\in\tilde{B}
and
y\in\tilde{A}
. We say that a a special edge
e\in E(G)
is
bad
, if some edge of
P^{*}(e)
lies in
E_{H}(\tilde{A},\tilde{B})
. Clearly, the number of bad special edges is bounded by
|E_{H}(\tilde{A},\tilde{B})|\leq\frac{|T|}{8\log^{6}N}
. Recall that, since cut
(\tilde{A},\tilde{B})
is finalizing, one of the sides of the cut
Z\in\left\{\tilde{A},\tilde{B}\right\}
contains at least
|V(H)|-\frac{\hat{W}}{32}
vertices of
H
, and at most
|T|\cdot\left(1-\frac{7}{\log^{4}N}\right)
vertices of
T
.

We start with
(\tilde{A}^{\prime},\tilde{B}^{\prime})=(\tilde{A},\tilde{B})
, and then modify the cut
(\tilde{A}^{\prime},\tilde{B}^{\prime})
, in two steps. In the first step, we consider every bad edge
e\in E(G)
in turn. If
e\in E^{A}\cup E^{AB}\cup E^{BA}
, then we move all inner vertices of
P^{*}(e)
to set
\tilde{A}^{\prime}
. Otherwise,
e\in E^{B}
must hold, and we move all inner vertices of
P^{*}(e)
to
\tilde{B}^{\prime}
.

Consider the resulting cut
(\tilde{A}^{\prime},\tilde{B}^{\prime})
, that is obtained after every bad special edge of
G
has been processed.
It is immediate to verify that
|E_{H}(\tilde{A}^{\prime},\tilde{B}^{\prime})|\leq|E_{H}(\tilde{A},\tilde{B})|\leq\frac{|T|}{8\log^{6}N}
continues to hold. Indeed, every bad edge
e\in E(G)
contributes at least one edge of
P^{*}(e)
to
E_{H}(\tilde{A},\tilde{B})
, and it contributes at most one edge of
P^{*}(e)
to
E_{H}(\tilde{A}^{\prime},\tilde{B}^{\prime})
.
It is immediate to verify that every edge in
E_{H}(\tilde{A}^{\prime},\tilde{B}^{\prime})
is a special edge, since, for every special edge
e
of
G
, the first and the last edge on path
P^{*}(e)
are special edges of
H
.
Moreover, every edge in
E_{H}(\tilde{A}^{\prime},\tilde{B}^{\prime})
is a special edge that is a child-edge of some bad edge in
E^{AB}
.

Recall that the number of bad edges in
G
is bounded by
|E_{H}(\tilde{A},\tilde{B})|\leq\frac{|T|}{8\log^{6}N}
.
Consider the set
Z\in\left\{\tilde{A},\tilde{B}\right\}
, such that contains at least
|V(H)|-\frac{\hat{W}}{32}
vertices of
H
, and at most
|T|\cdot\left(1-\frac{7}{\log^{4}N}\right)
vertices of
T
. Since Event
{\cal{E}}_{4}
did not happen, for every bad edge
e\in E(G)
, the correpsonding path
P^{*}(e)
contains at most
16\log N
vertices of
T
.
Therefore, the total number of vertices of
T
that our procedure could have added to
Z
during the first step is bounded by
\frac{|T|}{8\log^{6}N}\cdot 16\log N\leq\frac{2|T|}{\log^{5}N}
. Therefore, after the transformation,
Z
contains at most
|T|\cdot\left(1-\frac{6}{\log^{4}N}\right)
vertices of
T
.
Additionally, since the length of every edge
e
in
G
is bounded by
d^{\prime}/2
, for every special edge
e\in E(G)
, the number of vertices on path
P^{*}(e)
is bounded by
d^{\prime}
. Therefore, the total number of vertices that our algorithm may have removed from
Z
is bounded by
d^{\prime}\cdot\frac{|T|}{8\log^{6}N}\leq\frac{\hat{W}}{\log^{6}N}
(since
|T|\leq\frac{8\hat{W}}{d^{\prime}}
, as Event
{\cal{E}}_{1}
did not happen). Therefore, after the first step,
Z
still contains at least
|V(H)|-\frac{3\hat{W}}{64}
vertices. To summarize, after the first step,
Z
contains at least
|V(H)|-\frac{3\hat{W}}{64}
vertices, and at most
|T|\cdot\left(1-\frac{6}{\log^{4}N}\right)
vertices of
T
.

Consider any special edge
e=(x,y)
of
G
. If
x,y\in\tilde{A}^{\prime}
, and any vertex of
P^{*}(e)
originally lied in
\tilde{B}^{\prime}
, then
e
must have been a bad edge, as some edge on
P^{*}(e)
must have lied in
E_{H}(\tilde{A},\tilde{B})
. Our algorithm then moved all inner vertices on
P^{*}(e)
to
\tilde{A}^{\prime}
. Simiarly, if
x,y\in\tilde{B}^{\prime}
, then our algorithm ensures that all inner vertices on
P^{*}(e)
lie in
\tilde{B}^{\prime}
. If
x\in\tilde{A}
and
y\in\tilde{B}
held, then
e
must be a bad edge, since some edge on
P^{*}(e)
must lie in
E_{H}(\tilde{A},\tilde{B})
. Our algorithm moved all inner vertices of
P^{*}(e)
to
\tilde{A}^{\prime}
. Lastly, assume that
x\in\tilde{B}^{\prime}
and
y\in\tilde{A}^{\prime}
holds. If
e
is a bad edge, then we have moved all inner vertices of
P^{*}(e)
to
\tilde{A}^{\prime}
. It now only remains to take care of the subset
E^{*}\subseteq E^{BA}
of special edges, that contains every edge
e\in E^{BA}
that is not bad. We take care of these edges in the second step.

Intuitively, in the second step, we consider each edge
e\in E^{*}
one by one, and for each such edge, we move all inner vertices of
P^{*}(e)
to one side of the cut. However, we need to be careful so that we do not remove too many vertices from the set
Z
, and that we do not add too many vertices of
T
to
Z
.

We define a subset
E^{*}_{1}\subseteq E^{*}
of edges to contain every edge
e\in E^{*}
, such that the path
P^{*}(e)
contains at least one vertex of
T
. If
|E^{*}_{1}|>\left\lceil\frac{6|T|}{\log^{4}N}\right\rceil
, then we discard edges arbitrarily from
E^{*}_{1}
, until
|E^{*}_{1}|=\left\lceil\frac{6|T|}{\log^{4}N}\right\rceil
holds.

We now consider two cases. The first case happens when
Z=\tilde{A}
.
In this case, for every edge
e\in E^{*}_{1}
, we move all inner vertices on path
P^{*}(e)
to
\tilde{B}^{\prime}
, and for every edge
e\in E^{*}\setminus E^{*}_{1}
, we move all inner vertices on path
P^{*}(e)
to
\tilde{A}^{\prime}
. Notice that, if
|E^{*}_{1}|=\frac{6|T|}{\log^{4}N}
, then this process guarantees that
|\tilde{B}^{\prime}\cap T|\geq\frac{6|T|}{\log^{4}N}
holds at the end of the second step, and so
|\tilde{A}^{\prime}\cap T|\leq|T|\cdot\left(1-\frac{6}{\log^{4}N}\right)
holds. If
|E^{*}_{1}|<\frac{6|T|}{\log^{4}N}
, then we did not add any new terminals to set
\tilde{A}^{\prime}
in this step, and so
|\tilde{A}^{\prime}\cap T|\leq|T|\cdot\left(1-\frac{6}{\log^{4}N}\right)
continues to hold. Since, for every edge
e\in E^{*}_{1}
, the number of inner vertices on path
P^{*}(e)
is bounded by
d^{\prime}
, the number of vertices that we have deleted from
\tilde{A}^{\prime}
in this step is bounded by:

d^{\prime}\cdot|E^{*}_{1}|\leq d^{\prime}\cdot\left\lceil\frac{6|T|}{\log^{4}N}\right\rceil\leq d^{\prime}\cdot\frac{100\hat{W}}{d^{\prime}\log^{4}N}\leq\frac{\hat{W}}{\log^{3}N}.

(We have used the fact that
|T|\leq\frac{8\hat{W}}{d^{\prime}}
, since Event
{\cal{E}}_{1}
did not happen).
Therefore, after the second step,
\tilde{A}^{\prime}
contains at least
|V(H)|-\frac{3\hat{W}}{64}-\frac{\hat{W}}{\log^{3}N}\geq|V(H)|-\frac{\hat{W}}{16}
vertices.

If
Z=\tilde{B}
, then for every edge
e\in E^{*}_{1}
, we move all inner vertices on path
P^{*}(e)
to
\tilde{A}^{\prime}
, and for every edge
e\in E^{*}\setminus E^{*}_{1}
, we move all inner vertices on path
P^{*}(e)
to
\tilde{B}^{\prime}
. Using the same arguments as before, we conclude that, after the second step,
|\tilde{B}^{\prime}\cap T|\leq|T|\cdot\left(1-\frac{6}{\log^{4}N}\right)
and
|\tilde{B}^{\prime}|\geq|V(H)|-\frac{\hat{W}}{8}
hold.

It is easy to verify that the time required to compute the modified cut
(\tilde{A}^{\prime},\tilde{B}^{\prime})
is bounded by
O(|E(H)|)\leq O(\hat{W}^{2})
.

D.3
Proof of
Observation

7.14

It is enough to prove that inequalities
r\leq\left\lceil\sqrt{\log N^{\prime}}\right\rceil
,
1\leq\eta^{\prime}\leq\hat{\Delta}
, and
\frac{(|V(H)|-|B|)\cdot\eta^{\prime}}{\hat{\Delta}}\leq 2^{r\sqrt{\log N^{\prime}}}
all hold.

First, since
r\leq\left\lceil\sqrt{\log N}\right\rceil
, and
N\leq N^{\prime}
, it is immediate to verify that
r\leq\left\lceil\sqrt{\log N^{\prime}}\right\rceil
.

Second, since Event
{\cal{E}}_{1}
did not happen,
\hat{\Delta}\geq\frac{|T|}{4}\geq\frac{\hat{W}}{8d^{\prime}}
.
Since
\eta^{\prime}=(\log N^{\prime})^{8}
, we then get that:

\begin{split}\hat{\Delta}\geq\frac{\hat{W}}{8d^{\prime}}\geq\frac{n}{16d^{\prime}}\geq\frac{n\cdot(\log N)^{28}}{32d}\geq\frac{(\log N)^{28}}{64}\geq\eta^{\prime}.\end{split}

(we have used the fact that
\hat{W}\geq|V(G)|\geq\frac{n}{2}
;
d^{\prime}\leq\frac{2d}{(\log N)^{28}}
;
d\leq n
from Inequaltiy
15
), and
\eta^{\prime}=(\log N^{\prime})^{8}
. Therefore,
1\leq\eta^{\prime}\leq\hat{\Delta}
holds.

Lastly:

\frac{(|V(H)|-|B|)\cdot\eta^{\prime}}{\hat{\Delta}}\leq\frac{\hat{W}\cdot\eta^{\prime}}{\hat{\Delta}}\leq 8d^{\prime}(\log N^{\prime})^{8}\leq d\leq 2^{r\cdot\sqrt{\log N}}\leq 2^{r\cdot\sqrt{\log N^{\prime}}}.

D.4
Proof of
Claim

7.15

We start by turning the cut
(Z,Z^{\prime})
into a weakly well-structured one, using the following straightforward algorithm. Consider every vertex
v\in Z
one by one. If there is a regular edge
(v,u)\in E_{H}(Z,Z^{\prime})
, then we move
v
to
Z^{\prime}
. Notice that, if a regular edge
(v,u)
exists, then
v\in L
must hold, so moving
v
to
Z^{\prime}
may introduce at most one new edge into the set
E_{H}(Z,Z^{\prime})
– the special edge that is incident to
v
. At the same time, at least one regular edge is deleted from
E_{H}(Z,Z^{\prime})
, so
|E_{H}(Z,Z^{\prime})|
does not increase. At the end of this procedure, cut
(Z,Z^{\prime})
becomes weakly well-structured, and
|E_{H}(Z,Z^{\prime})|\leq\frac{\hat{W}}{d^{\prime}(\log N)^{7}}
continues to hold. Since
|T|\geq\frac{\hat{W}}{2d^{\prime}}
, as Event
{\cal{E}}_{1}
did not happen, we get that
|E_{H}(Z,Z^{\prime})|\leq\frac{2|T|}{\log^{7}N}
. The number of vertices that were deleted from
Z
is bounded by the original number of edges in
E_{H}(Z,Z^{\prime})
, which is bounded by
\frac{\hat{W}}{d^{\prime}(\log N)^{7}}\leq\frac{2|T|}{\log^{7}N}\leq\frac{|T|}{8\log^{6}N}
. Therefore, at the end of this procedure,
|Z\cap T|,|Z^{\prime}\cap T|\geq\frac{7|T|}{8\log^{4}N}
holds. Notice that the running time of the algorithm so far is bounded by
O(|E(H)|)\leq O(\hat{W}^{2})
.
If either of the sets
Z
or
Z^{\prime}
contains at least
|V(H)|-\frac{\hat{W}}{32}=\frac{31\hat{W}}{32}
vertices, then cut
(Z,Z^{\prime})
is a finalizing cut (see
Definition

7.7
). We then terminate the algorithm and return this cut.

We assume from now on that
|Z|,|Z^{\prime}|\geq\frac{\hat{W}}{32}
. Since
|E_{H}(Z,Z^{\prime})|\leq\frac{\hat{W}}{d^{\prime}(\log N)^{7}}\leq\frac{32}{d^{\prime}\cdot(\log N)^{7}}\cdot\min\left\{|Z|,|Z^{\prime}|\right\}
, we get that the sparsity of the cut
(Z,Z^{\prime})
is at most
\frac{32}{d^{\prime}\cdot(\log N)^{7}}
.

Next, we turn the cut
(Z,Z^{\prime})
into a
cut
(A^{\prime},B^{\prime})
in
G
, in a natural way: we let
A^{\prime}=Z\cap V(G)
, and
B^{\prime}=Z^{\prime}\cap V(G)
.
Recall that the length of every edge in
G
is at most
\frac{d^{\prime}}{2}
. From
Observation

4.4
,
(A^{\prime},B^{\prime})
is a weakly well-structured cut in
G
of sparsity at most
\frac{256}{d^{\prime}\log^{7}N}
, with
w(A^{\prime})\geq\frac{Z}{8}\geq\frac{\hat{W}}{256}
and
w(B^{\prime})\geq\frac{|Z^{\prime}|}{8}\geq\frac{\hat{W}}{256}
. The time required to compute the cut
(A^{\prime},B^{\prime})
in
G
given the cut
(Z,Z^{\prime})
in
H
is bounded by
O(|V(H)|)\leq O(\hat{W})
.

Recall that for every special edge
e\in E(G)
,
\ell(e)\leq\frac{d^{\prime}}{2}\leq\frac{n}{16}
, while
\sum_{e\in E(G)}\ell(e)\geq\frac{n}{4}
.
We can now apply the algorithm from
Observation

4.5
to cut
(A^{\prime},B^{\prime})
, to compute a strongly well-structured cut
(A,B)
in
G
with
w(A)\geq\frac{w(A^{\prime})}{4}\geq\frac{\hat{W}}{1024}\geq\frac{\hat{W}}{\log^{30}N}
and
w(B)\geq\frac{w(B^{\prime})}{4}\geq\frac{\hat{W}}{\log^{30}N}
, whose sparsity is at most
\frac{1024}{d^{\prime}\log^{7}N}\leq\frac{1}{d^{\prime}}
.
The running time of the algorithm from
Observation

4.5
is
O(|E(H)|)\leq O(\hat{W}^{2})
. We then return the cut
(A,B)
.

From the above discussion, the total running time of the algorithm is
O(\hat{W})^{2}
.

D.5
Proof of
Claim

7.21

Clearly,
G
is a perfect well-structured graph, and the assignment
\ell^{\prime}(e)
of lengths to its edges
e\in E(G)
is a proper assignment. The current weight of all vertices in
G
with respect to the edge lengths
\ell^{\prime}(\cdot)
is
W^{\prime}\leq 2\hat{W}\leq 24W^{0}
, while
N\geq W^{0}
holds from the definition of the
\mathsf{MaintainCluster}
problem. Therefore,
N^{\prime}=8N\geq 8W^{0}\geq 8n\geq 8|V(G)|
, and
N^{\prime}=8N\geq 8W^{0}\geq\frac{W^{\prime}}{8}
holds, so
N^{\prime}\geq\max\left\{|V(G)|,\frac{W^{\prime}}{8}\right\}
, as required.
Since
N
is large enough from the definition of the
\mathsf{MaintainCluster}
problem, so is
N^{\prime}
.
Since Event
{\cal{E}}_{1}
did not happen,
|\beta|=|V(X)|=|T|\geq\frac{\hat{W}}{2d^{\prime}}\geq\frac{W^{\prime}}{256d^{\prime}}
, as required.
Since
1\leq r\leq\left\lceil\sqrt{\log N}\right\rceil
holds from the definition of
r
-restricted
\mathsf{MaintainCluster}
problem, and
N^{\prime}\geq N
, we get that
1\leq r\leq\left\lceil\sqrt{\log N^{\prime}}\right\rceil
.
Lastly, we need to verify that
d^{\prime}\leq\min\left\{\frac{2^{r\sqrt{\log N^{\prime}}}}{\log^{8}N^{\prime}},\frac{|V(G)|}{8}\right\}
holds.
Indeed, recall that
d^{\prime}\leq\frac{2d}{(\log N)^{28}}
. Moreover, from Inequality

15
,
d\leq n
, and from the definition of the
r
-restricted
\mathsf{MaintainCluster}
problem,

d\leq 2^{r\cdot\sqrt{\log N}}
. Therefore:

\begin{split}d^{\prime}&\leq\frac{2d}{(\log N)^{28}}\\
&\leq\min\left\{\frac{2n}{(\log N)^{28}},\frac{2\cdot 2^{r\cdot\sqrt{\log N}}}{(\log N)^{28}}\right\}\\
&\leq\min\left\{\frac{2^{30}\cdot n}{(\log N^{\prime})^{28}},\frac{2^{29}\cdot 2^{r\cdot\sqrt{\log N^{\prime}}}}{(\log N^{\prime})^{28}}\right\}\\
&\leq\min\left\{\frac{|V(G)|}{8},\frac{2^{r\sqrt{\log N^{\prime}}}}{\log^{8}N^{\prime}}\right\}.\end{split}

(We have used the fact that, from Inequality
17
,
\log N^{\prime}\leq 2\log N
).
